  <div  class="popup-content">
    
<form class="search-form" role="search" method="get" id="" action="https://www.group-ib.com/">
  <input type="search" placeholder="Search" name="s" id="search-input" value=""/>
  <button type="submit" class="search-button"><img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/search-icon.svg" alt="Search Icon"></button>
</form>   </div>

<style>
  .hidden {
    display: none;
  }
</style>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
  const openSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.open-search-popup')];
  const closeSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.close-search-popup')];
  const searchPopup = document.querySelector('.search-popup');

  const closePopup = () => {
    searchPopup.style.display = 'none';
    openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.add('hidden'));
  };

  openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(openSearchPopupBtn => {
    openSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', (e) => {
      e.preventDefault();

      searchPopup.style.display = 'block';
      openSearchPopupBtn.classList.add('hidden');
      closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    });
  });

  closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(closeSearchPopupBtn => {
    closeSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', () => {
      closePopup();
    });
  });

  document.addEventListener('click', (event) => {
    if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target) && !openSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target)) && !closeSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target))) {
      setTimeout(() => {
        if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target)) {
          closePopup();
        }
      }, 100);
    }
  });
</script>    </div>

                            <h1 class="header__title title-h2 mb-24 mt-md-48 mt-lg-64 mt-xl-64">Shedding light on the dark web</h1><p class="header__text mb-xs-48 mb-64 mb-lg-96 mb-xl-96">Cybersecurity analyst's guide on how to use machine learning to show cybercriminals' true colors</p><div class="post-info__date mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><div class="body2">January 28, 2022 · <span id="reading-time"></span> min to read · Cyber Investigations</div></div><div class="post-info__image mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><picture><source srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-51.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img data-no-lazy="" fetchpriority="high" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-51.jpg" alt="" class="webpexpress-processed"></picture></div><div class="post-info__tags"><div class="post-info__tag body2">Dark web</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">Investigations</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">Machine learning</div></div>                        </div>
                    </div>
                </div>
                <div class="blog-post-content" id="blog-post-content">
                    <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h2>Introduction</h2>
<p><strong>Data leaks</strong> appearing on the <a href="https://www.group-ib.com/resources/knowledge-hub/dark-web/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">dark web</a> are an actual problem of the modern world. Events of the recent past show that even <strong>world-renowned enterprise, financial and IT giants are not immune to data leaks</strong>. Equifax, British Airways, SingHealth, Marriott International, Sephora, Canva, Zynga, Microsoft, Tokopedia, T-Mobile, Linkedin, Twitch — these are just a few names that have been spotted in high-profile data breach scandals in the past several years. Оne would hardly attempt to question if these companies had enough resources to ensure their security, but here is the fact — they all did fall victim to cyberattacks.</p>
<p>Even if the company&#8217;s data was leaked not as a result of its own actions, but rather the actions of its contractor or a partner with which it shared data, the result will be the same. Data leaks can both disclose sensitive information about the company&#8217;s internal processes affecting business decisions or reputation and personal data of the company&#8217;s customers, making them turn their back on a brand or company that used to be their favorite. Both outcomes can bring the business at the verge of collapse, which is why one of the first instincts of breached companies, burning from thirst for revenge, is to find attackers behind the network compromise and data leak to dispense justice.</p>
<p>These <strong>data leaks can often end in the shadow part of the Internet</strong>. Underground resources, which are closed to unauthorized visitors, are rife with discussions that are directly related to planned or previously committed crimes. These resource regulars are attackers themselves, their accomplices looking for an opportunity to make some money, but also visitors who have their own agenda, though, distinct from others. This is the case for employees of law enforcement agencies and special services, as well as corporate and private security specialists. Under the guise of bad guys, they collect cyber intelligence data and research and analyze criminal activity in order to investigate and prevent cybercrimes.</p>
<p>An average underground forum has huge volumes of daily text messages going through it, which makes analyzing this information manually almost impossible and also ineffective. <strong>Algorithm development and machine learning</strong> (hereinafter &#8211; ML) implementation can break this stalemate, significantly reducing the amount of manual work and enabling analysts to connect the dots in the tremendous amount of data.</p>
<p>This article aims to show the methods cybersecurity analysts who come to the aid of compromised companies in such cases can use to, firstly, determine if an alleged data breach did take place and a database put up for sale in the dark web was authentic, and, secondly, identify the threat actor responsible. It demonstrates how machine learning algorithms can facilitate the processes of cyber intelligence data analysis and cyber investigations, while at the same time further enriching its results. And if it happens that you somehow embarked on the path of investigating a data leak, this guide will give you the ideas from what to begin and how to further proceed.</p>
<p><strong>This guide is intended for:</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><strong>cybersecurity greeners</strong> who are doing their first steps in the cybersecurity world. Even if not all the terms and techniques described in the text are familiar to you, you can get your first impression of cyber investigation process and strengthen your intention to pursue a cybersecurity career;</li>
<li><strong>cybersecurity analysts and corporate security team members</strong>. From the text you&#8217;ll learn the methods that can be used to probe into a data leak, even if for the time being you&#8217;re sure that your customers are reliably protected;</li>
<li><strong>machine learning algorithm developers</strong> who will get a broader perspective of the cybercrime investigation process and be able to apply this knowledge in the future to advance the cybercrime investigation industry, making the process more efficient and prompt.</li>
</ul>
<p>Thus, we&#8217;ll focus on <strong>two major points</strong>:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>Real breach or fake</strong>. There are leaks that are purported to be new to the public, but in reality they turn out to be databases that were earlier released somewhere else or are new only to some extent, comprising both old and fresh data.</li>
<li><strong>Skilled threat actor or a newbie</strong>. Many attackers today use multiple accounts on the underground forums in order to better hide their activities. To determine the goal of an attack and proceed with its further probe, one has to know the adversary.</li>
</ol>
<h2>Manual analysis</h2>
<p>We decided to demonstrate the methods that can be used for the achievement of the aforesaid goals using a case with <strong>RaidForums</strong> underground platform where a set of databases was released on September 17, 2020 by a user nicknamed <strong>ExpertData</strong>.</p>
<div id="attachment_25476-2" style="width: 1290px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-9.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25476-2" decoding="async" class="wp-image-25476 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201280%20498'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of ExpertData's post offering for sale a set of databases posted on RaidForums" width="1280" height="498" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-9.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25476-2" decoding="async" class="wp-image-25476 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-9.png" alt="Screenshot of ExpertData's post offering for sale a set of databases posted on RaidForums" width="1280" height="498"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25476-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 1 &#8211; Screenshot of ExpertData&#8217;s post offering for sale a set of databases posted on RaidForums (currently deleted; screenshot taken from https://twitter.com/Bank_Security)</p></div>
<p>First, Group-IB analysts began to manually analyze the <strong>ExpertData</strong> account. The information publicly available on the forum indicates that this user joined RaidForums on September 5, 2020.</p>
<div id="attachment_25477-2" style="width: 1713px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-10.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25477-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25477 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201703%20815'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="ExpertData's profile on RaidForums" width="1703" height="815" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-10.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25477-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25477 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-10.png" alt="ExpertData's profile on RaidForums" width="1703" height="815"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25477-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 2 &#8211; ExpertData&#8217;s profile on RaidForums (captured in November 2020, the label &#8220;Scammer&#8221; was added by the forum admins after several claims against ExpertData. This label was then removed after the disputes were resolved)</p></div>
<p>The account was then renamed to repeat its user identifier 121907651 (several threads and posts of ExpertData were removed by admins, which is why the total number of posts differs from fig.2).</p>
<div id="attachment_25478-2" style="width: 1262px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-11.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25478-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25478 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201252%20830'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="ExpertData's profile on RaidForums " width="1252" height="830" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-11.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25478-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25478 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-11.png" alt="ExpertData's profile on RaidForums " width="1252" height="830"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25478-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 3 &#8211; ExpertData&#8217;s profile on RaidForums (captured in September 2021)</p></div>
<p>So the situation was quite typical — a new user published information about multiple data leaks from companies in different regions. It was reasonable to doubt if the databases were real leaks and if the attacker could have been trusted. Then we started to analyze threads of discussion where this account participated.</p>
<p>On September 17, 2020, <strong>ExpertData</strong> published a thread to sell a leaked database, allegedly containing info on over 100,000 users.</p>
<div id="attachment_25479-2" style="width: 2672px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-11.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25479-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25479 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%202662%201606'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="ExpertData's post offering for sale a database" width="2662" height="1606" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-11.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25479-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25479 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-11.png" alt="ExpertData's post offering for sale a database" width="2662" height="1606"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25479-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 4 &#8211; ExpertData&#8217;s post offering for sale a database</p></div>
<p>On the same day, ExpertData published a thread to sell 18 databases leaked from companies in Brazil, Egypt, Indonesia, India, Italy, Hong Kong, Mexico, Saudi Arabia, Singapore, Thailand, Vietnam, UK, US, and other countries (fig.1). As it can be seen from the screenshot, the original post didn&#8217;t mention the names of the companies compromised.</p>
<p>About a month later, on October 28, 2020, ExpertData published the same post, this time specifying the names of the companies breached and excluding Saudi Arabian companies in the travel industry from the list.</p>
<div id="attachment_25480-2" style="width: 1687px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-10.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25480-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25480 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201677%20533'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of ExpertData's post offering for sale a set of databases" width="1677" height="533" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-10.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25480-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25480 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-10.png" alt="Screenshot of ExpertData's post offering for sale a set of databases" width="1677" height="533"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25480-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 5 &#8211; Screenshot of ExpertData&#8217;s post offering for sale a set of databases mentioning the names of the companies compromised</p></div>
<p>In the post ExpertData has also specified his contact details: Jabber account <em><a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection" class="__cf_email__" data-cfemail="57322f2732252333362336173d3635353225792522">[email&#160;protected]</a></em> and Telegram account <em>@ExpertData</em>.</p>
<div id="attachment_25481-2" style="width: 1210px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-2.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25481-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25481 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201200%20675'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="ExpertData's account on Telegram" width="1200" height="675" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-2.jpg"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25481-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25481 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-2.jpg" alt="ExpertData's account on Telegram" width="1200" height="675"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25481-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 6 &#8211; ExpertData&#8217;s account on Telegram</p></div>
<p>The databases published by this author seemed to be unique since they had not been seen in the publications of other threat actors before. This was also noted by other forum members, who commented on one of ExpertData&#8217;s posts.</p>
<div id="attachment_25482-2" style="width: 1315px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-10.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25482-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25482 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201305%20793'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="screenshot of comments on ExpertData's post" width="1305" height="793" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-10.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25482-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25482 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-10.png" alt="screenshot of comments on ExpertData's post" width="1305" height="793"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25482-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 7 &#8211; One of the active forum users with a high reputation commenting on ExpertData&#8217;s post</p></div>
<p>In early November 2020, a user nicknamed <strong>The Polaris</strong> initiated a dispute against ExpertData in a separate thread. The Polaris, who attempted to purchase a database from ExpertData, then accused the latter of lying about the quality (quantity of correct password hashes) of the NitroGo (GoNitro.com) dump in a bid to get the payment worth $10,000.</p>
<p>The dispute thread was published on November 2, 2020. At the time of writing, the thread is deleted, but Group-IB analysts managed to retrieve it.</p>
<div id="attachment_25483-2" style="width: 1749px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-8.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25483-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25483 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201739%20927'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of the dispute thread against ExpertData" width="1739" height="927" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-8.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25483-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25483 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-8.png" alt="Screenshot of the dispute thread against ExpertData" width="1739" height="927"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25483-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 8 &#8211; Screenshot of the dispute thread against ExpertData initiated by a user nicknamed The Polaris</p></div>
<p><strong>The Polaris</strong> joined Raidforums on August 29, 2020, six days before ExpertData account was created.</p>
<div id="attachment_25484-2" style="width: 1082px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-8.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25484-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25484 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201072%20638'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="The Polaris' account on RaidForums" width="1072" height="638" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-8.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25484-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25484 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-8.png" alt="The Polaris' account on RaidForums" width="1072" height="638"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25484-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 9 &#8211; The Polaris&#8217; account on RaidForums</p></div>
<p>Surprisingly, ExpertData wasn&#8217;t the first to reply to this dispute: a user with the nickname ShinyHunters stood up for him and came up with counter accusations against The Polaris.</p>
<div id="attachment_25485-2" style="width: 947px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-7.png.webp 937w, https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-7-150x150.png.webp 150w" sizes="(max-width: 937px) 100vw, 937px" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25485-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25485 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20937%20939'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of the dispute thread against ExpertData showing ShinyHunters standing up for the former" width="937" height="939" data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-7.png 937w, https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-7-150x150.png 150w" data-lazy-sizes="(max-width: 937px) 100vw, 937px" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-7.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25485-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25485 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-7.png" alt="Screenshot of the dispute thread against ExpertData showing ShinyHunters standing up for the former" width="937" height="939" srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-7.png 937w, https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-7-150x150.png 150w" sizes="(max-width: 937px) 100vw, 937px"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25485-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 10 &#8211; Screenshot of the dispute thread against ExpertData showing ShinyHunters standing up for the former</p></div>
<p>In their post, <strong>ShinyHunters</strong> accused <strong>The Polaris</strong> of hiding under multiple accounts and included a link to a screenshot of their earlier conversation in Jabber, in which ShinyHunters suspected that it might be cybersecurity researcher Vinny Troia hiding behind the user nicknamed The Polaris, and welcomed him in a personal chat as &#8220;Hello Vinny.&#8221;</p>
<div id="attachment_25486-2" style="width: 1338px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-min-4.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25486-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25486 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201328%20554'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of a chat between The Polaris and ShinyHunters in Jabber" width="1328" height="554" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-min-4.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25486-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25486 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-min-4.png" alt="Screenshot of a chat between The Polaris and ShinyHunters in Jabber" width="1328" height="554"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25486-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 11 &#8211; Screenshot of a chat between The Polaris and ShinyHunters in Jabber</p></div>
<p>ShinyHunters are a famous group of hackers who intrude their victim networks and exfiltrate data. The group with this name became famous in the APAC region after Tokopedia leak in May 2020, and is famous for selling unique databases that they exfiltrate during their hacking activity.</p>
<p>Vinny Troia is, in particular, known for researching hacker group ShinyHunters and having published in July 2020 a <a href="https://nightlion.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/12/The-Dark-Overlord-Investigation-Report-Night-Lion_v1.01.pdf" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">research</a> about them. According to his findings, it is supposed that ShinyHunters is a successor to NSFW and Gnostic Players groups.</p>
<div id="attachment_25487-2" style="width: 945px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/12-min-4.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25487-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25487 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20935%20419'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of ShinyHunters' post" width="935" height="419" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/12-min-4.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25487-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25487 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/12-min-4.png" alt="Screenshot of ShinyHunters' post" width="935" height="419"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25487-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 12 &#8211; Screenshot of ShinyHunters&#8217; post, in which they claim that The Polaris is multi accounting and has another profile on the forum</p></div>
<p>The dispute stopped on November 10, 2020. <strong>The Polaris</strong> was banned. Moreover, the account of <strong>ExpertData</strong> was labeled as a scam one due to several disputes on the forum. After the disputes were resolved, this label was removed from the ExpertData account, which might indicate that the claims of The Polaris were not deemed relevant by forum administrators.</p>
<p>Typically, the <strong>ShinyHunters</strong> account doesn&#8217;t join any disputes not against them, which is why we suggested that there might be a link between ShinyHunters and ExpertData. To further explore this assumption, we started comparing the threads, in which these two users participated and their posts themselves.</p>
<p>After the post with 18 private databases, ExpertData published two more databases (now the posts are deleted from RaidForums): GoNitro (on October 29, 2020) and AnimalJam (on November 11, 2020).</p>
<p>On October 21, 2020, the Nitro PDF (sold as GoNitro dump) service stated that it had been subject to a low impact security incident, however, the media then <a href="https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/massive-nitro-data-breach-impacts-microsoft-google-apple-more/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">reported</a> that user and document databases allegedly stolen as a result of the incident were offered for sale in a private auction. ExpertData published information about the GoNitro databases on October 29, 2020, which seems to be the first public post offering for sale the databases.</p>
<p>Later, in January 2021, GoNitro was republished again by the user nicknamed <strong>Spiral</strong> with a reference to <strong>ShinyHunters</strong>.</p>
<div id="attachment_25429-2" style="width: 976px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/13-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25429-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25429 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20966%20555'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of Spiral's post offering for sale the GoNitro dump" width="966" height="555" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/13-min-2.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25429-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25429 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/13-min-2.png" alt="Screenshot of Spiral's post offering for sale the GoNitro dump" width="966" height="555"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25429-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 13 &#8211; Screenshot of Spiral&#8217;s post offering for sale the GoNitro dump</p></div>
<p><strong>Thus, we can suggest that ExpertData had access to the leaked GoNitro databases because of the connection with ShinyHunters.</strong></p>
<p>On November 10, 2020, user <strong>Chandler-Bing</strong> published the <strong>AnimalJam</strong> database with a reference to <strong>ShinyHunters</strong> (the original post has been modified by the forum admins,and now can only be found in the reply of another user).</p>
<div id="attachment_25430-2" style="width: 1021px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/14-min-5.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25430-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25430 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201011%20513'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Commentary showing the offer to sell the base by Chandler-Bing" width="1011" height="513" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/14-min-5.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25430-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25430 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/14-min-5.png" alt="Commentary showing the offer to sell the base by Chandler-Bing" width="1011" height="513"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25430-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 14 &#8211; Commentary showing the offer to sell the base by Chandler-Bing</p></div>
<p><strong>ExpertData</strong> then published the same database a day later, on November 11, 2020. This could also be a sign of a connection between <strong>ExpertData</strong> and <strong>ShinyHunters</strong>.</p>
<p>However, it is also possible that <strong>ExpertData</strong> just bought the databases and published them by himself. To confirm or reject our hypothesis about affiliation between <strong>ExpertData</strong> and <strong>ShinyHunters</strong>, we decided to analyze <strong>ExpertData</strong> account history.</p>
<p>It was revealed that back in March 2020 the user with the nickname <strong>Expert</strong> used the same Telegram account <em>@expertdata</em> (later in March 2020, the account was banned).</p>
<div id="attachment_25431-2" style="width: 1029px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min-3.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25431-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25431 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201019%20482'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of Expert's post" width="1019" height="482" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min-3.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25431-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25431 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min-3.png" alt="Screenshot of Expert's post" width="1019" height="482"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25431-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 15 &#8211; Screenshot of Expert&#8217;s post offering for sale a database</p></div>
<p><strong>We compared messages of users Expert and ExpertData and it seems that they used a similar message template to publish the leaks, except for one case only</strong> — the database published on September 17, 2020 (fig.4). The Expert account also focused on selling databases. However, the quality of databases posted by Expert and ExpertData (created in September 2020) accounts is different. Most of Expert&#8217;s databases were not authentic and represented combo lists of previously published data leaks. The changes in the quality of data offered for sale prompted us to think that the user might have started to work with some group in September 2020, presumably ShinyHunters.</p>
<p>We moved on with the messages of ShinyHunters and found another dispute on RaidForums, in which ShinyHunters was previously involved. On May 13, 2020, a user nicknamed <strong>Jumbo</strong> (account created on May 12, 2020) initiated a dispute against ShinyHunters and <strong>fs0c131y/whysodank</strong>. Jumbo claimed that he transferred 1.5 BTC to ShinyHunters for the databases put up for sale, but didn&#8217;t get the purchase, with ShinyHunters denying the money receipt.</p>
<div id="attachment_25432-2" style="width: 1419px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/16-min-4.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25432-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25432 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201409%20777'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of a dispute talk initiated by Jumbo against ShinyHunters" width="1409" height="777" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/16-min-4.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25432-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25432 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/16-min-4.png" alt="Screenshot of a dispute talk initiated by Jumbo against ShinyHunters" width="1409" height="777"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25432-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 16 &#8211; Screenshot of a dispute talk initiated by Jumbo against ShinyHunters</p></div>
<p>The account <strong>whysodank</strong>, mentioned in a dispute, was created back in April 2020.</p>
<div id="attachment_25433-2" style="width: 957px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/17-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25433-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25433 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20947%20577'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Whysodankk profile on RaidForums" width="947" height="577" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/17-min-2.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25433-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25433 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/17-min-2.png" alt="Whysodankk profile on RaidForums" width="947" height="577"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25433-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 17 &#8211; Whysodankk profile on RaidForums</p></div>
<p>The history of changing usernames in the profile is provided below.</p>
<div id="attachment_25434-2" style="width: 865px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25434-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25434 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20855%20195'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of previous nicknames of the whysodank account" width="855" height="195" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min-2.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25434-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25434 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min-2.png" alt="Screenshot of previous nicknames of the whysodank account" width="855" height="195"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25434-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 18 &#8211; Screenshot of previous nicknames of the whysodank account</p></div>
<p>A user with the nickname <strong>whysodankk</strong> joined the dispute and refuted <strong>Jumbo</strong>&#8216;s claims saying that the screenshots allegedly confirming the money transfer in fact represented the transfer of money between two wallets belonging to Jumbo. Whysodankk also confirmed that he knew ShinyHunters and supported his position in the dispute.</p>
<p>Jumbo participated in only one thread on the forum — with accusations against ShinyHunters. It is noteworthy that it was whysodankk who was mainly involved in the discussion, and not ShinyHunters, against whom the charges were brought as well.</p>
</div>

Screenshot of a post by whysodank supporting ShinyHunters in a dispute talk</p></div><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of a post by whysodank supporting ShinyHunters in a dispute talk" title="21-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-2.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-2.png" alt="Screenshot of a post by whysodank supporting ShinyHunters in a dispute talk" title="21-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text">Figure 21 - Screenshot of a post by whysodank supporting ShinyHunters in a dispute talk</p></div><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/22-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of a post by whysodank supporting ShinyHunters in a dispute talk" title="22-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/22-min-2.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/22-min-2.png" alt="Screenshot of a post by whysodank supporting ShinyHunters in a dispute talk" title="22-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text">Figure 22 - Screenshot of a post by whysodank supporting ShinyHunters in a dispute talk</p></div></div><div class="swiper-pagination"></div><div class="swiper-button-next"></div><div class="swiper-button-prev"></div></div></div>

                  <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>The dispute ended on May 14, 2020.<strong> Jumbo</strong> was banned from the forum over a fake scam report which shows that he didn&#8217;t provide evidence in support of his accusations against <strong>ShinyHunters</strong>.</p>
<p><strong>From the above posts it can be concluded that whysodankk is either a partner of ShinyHunters or just a different account of the latter.</strong> To check this, we decided to find accounts on the RaidForums whose contact details overlap with ShinyHunters&#8217; ones.</p>
<p><strong>It turns out that ShinyHunters and whysodankk specified the same contact details</strong> — <em><a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection" class="__cf_email__" data-cfemail="d6a5bebfb8afbea3b8a2b3a4a596aebba6a6f8bca6">[email&#160;protected]</a></em>. Examples of the posts are presented below.</p>
<div id="attachment_25439-2" style="width: 896px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/23-min-1.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25439-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25439 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20886%20441'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of ShinyHunters' post with Jabber account " width="886" height="441" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/23-min-1.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25439-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25439 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/23-min-1.png" alt="Screenshot of ShinyHunters' post with Jabber account " width="886" height="441"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25439-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 23 &#8211; Screenshot of ShinyHunters&#8217; post with Jabber account <a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection" class="__cf_email__" data-cfemail="017269686f7869746f7564737241796c71712f6b71">[email&#160;protected]</a></p></div>
<div id="attachment_25440-2" style="width: 1610px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/24-min-1.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25440-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25440 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201600%20545'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of whysodankk's post with Jabber account" width="1600" height="545" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/24-min-1.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25440-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25440 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/24-min-1.png" alt="Screenshot of whysodankk's post with Jabber account" width="1600" height="545"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25440-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 24 &#8211; Screenshot of whysodankk&#8217;s post with Jabber account <a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection" class="__cf_email__" data-cfemail="700318191e0918051e0415020330081d00005e1a00">[email&#160;protected]</a> (account whysodankk was used to sell the Tokopedia database back in May 2020. The post on the screenshot contains the first part of the dump, while the full dump of Tokopedia was published by this account later)</p></div>
<p>The same contact details indicate that ShinyHunters and whysodankk are related to each other. Moreover, these two accounts supported each other in the dispute.</p>
<p>The same ready-to-help strategy was used by ShinyHunters and ExpertData, which leads us to a conclusion that ExpertData might be a member or a data broker of ShinyHunters hacker group, and the databases trafficked by ExpertData could have belonged to ShinyHunters.</p>
<div id="attachment_25441-2" style="width: 1690px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/25-min.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25441-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25441 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201680%20900'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Timeline of disputes against ShinyHunters and ExpertData" width="1680" height="900" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/25-min.jpg"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25441-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25441 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/25-min.jpg" alt="Timeline of disputes against ShinyHunters and ExpertData" width="1680" height="900"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25441-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 25 &#8211; Timeline of disputes against ShinyHunters and ExpertData</p></div>
<p>Additional confirmation of this assumption can be found below.</p>
<div id="attachment_25442-2" style="width: 1253px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/26-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25442-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25442 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201243%20891'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of a post by The Polaris who alleged friendly links between users ShinyHunters and ExpertData" width="1243" height="891" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/26-min-2.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25442-2" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25442 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/26-min-2.png" alt="Screenshot of a post by The Polaris who alleged friendly links between users ShinyHunters and ExpertData" width="1243" height="891"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25442-2" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 26 &#8211; Screenshot of a post by The Polaris who alleged friendly links between users ShinyHunters and ExpertData</p></div>
